Elias sat at the hearth with Ada's letter folded on his knee. He had read it until the creases went soft. His mother's coat hung on its hook by the door.

He got up, fed the fire, sat down. Got up again and wiped a table that was already clean.

She had been kind to anyone who came to her door, and once she decided a thing, nothing turned her. A teacher, all the way through.

He took the coat down and held it a while, then laid it over her chair.

Ada was still in the Wailing Moth village, waiting for a sister who would not come.

Elias pulled paper toward him and began listing what a caravan would need to get through the passes.

snap-18-late-desperate · repeat 3 · Model · 119 words · 11.5 s

Elias checked the door latch twice, then a third time. Wren's Hollow was quiet. His mother's chair sat by the hearth, her coat over its back, and he did not move it.

On the table lay Ada's letter. He read it again, smoothing the folds flat with his thumb. Constance had been kind to everyone, and nothing had ever turned her aside once she decided. He was not like that. He worried; he made lists.

So he made one now. Warmer coats. More fuel. Sleds he could build himself, and build properly.

He folded the letter into his pocket. Ada was still waiting in the Wailing Moth village. He would finish what his mother started, and bring Ada home.
